STEP 45 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PYEOF'
path = 'sqlite_utils/db.py'
src = open(path).read()

block = '''
    def enable_safe_import(self) -> None:
        """
        Enable safe import mode for this database. While enabled the
        connection runs in autocommit mode and :meth:`create_import_checkpoint`
        can be used to create rollback checkpoints.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            self._safe_import_previous_isolation_level = self.conn.isolation_level
            self.conn.isolation_level = None
            self._safe_import_enabled = True

    def disable_safe_import(self) -> None:
        """
        Disable safe import mode for this database, restoring the previous
        transaction behaviour.
        """
        if self._safe_import_enabled:
            self.conn.isolation_level = self._safe_import_previous_isolation_level
            self._safe_import_enabled = False

    def _create_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        checkpoint_id = "sp_" + secrets.token_hex(16)
        self.execute(
            "SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id))
        )
        self._active_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
        return checkpoint_id

    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        """
        Create a rollback checkpoint and return its checkpoint id. The id is
        an opaque non-empty string.

        Raises :class:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode is not
        currently enabled.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError(
                "Safe import mode is not enabled - call enable_safe_import() first"
            )
        return self._create_checkpoint()

    def _require_active_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
            return
        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(
                "Checkpoint {} is no longer active".format(checkpoint_id)
            )
        raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
            "Checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
        )

    def commit_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Commit a checkpoint created using :meth:`create_import_checkpoint`.
        Any changes made since the checkpoint was created are kept.

        Committing finalizes the checkpoint: further commit or rollback calls
        with the same id will raise :class:`CheckpointNotActiveError`.
        """
        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)

    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Roll back to a checkpoint created using :meth:`create_import_checkpoint`,
        restoring the exact database state (including schema changes) from when
        the checkpoint was created.

        Rolling back finalizes the checkpoint: further commit or rollback calls
        with the same id will raise :class:`CheckpointNotActiveError`.
        """
        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        quoted = quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)

    def cleanup_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Remove a checkpoint id from tracking. Active checkpoints are released
        (their changes are kept) and finalized checkpoints are simply forgotten.

        After cleanup, commit/rollback calls with the same id raise
        :class:`CheckpointNotFoundError`.
        """
        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
            try:
                self.execute(
                    "RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id))
                )
            except Exception:
                pass
            self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        elif checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            self._finalized_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        else:
            raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
                "Checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
            )

    def _ensure_import_invariants_table(self) -> None:
        self.execute(
            _IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE_CREATE_SQL.format(
                quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
            )
        )

    def add_import_invariant(self, table: str, sql: str) -> str:
        """
        Add a persistent import invariant for a table. Returns an opaque
        invariant id.

        :param table: Name of the table the invariant applies to
        :param sql: SQL invariant expression, or a ``SELECT`` statement
        """
        self._ensure_import_invariants_table()
        invariant_id = "inv_" + secrets.token_hex(16)
        with self.conn:
            self.execute(
                "INSERT INTO {} (\\"table\\", id, expression) VALUES (?, ?, ?)".format(
                    quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
                ),
                (table, invariant_id, sql),
            )
        return invariant_id

    def remove_import_invariant(self, table: str, invariant_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Remove an import invariant for a table.

        :param table: Name of the table
        :param invariant_id: Id returned by :meth:`add_import_invariant`
        """
        self._ensure_import_invariants_table()
        with self.conn:
            self.execute(
                "DELETE FROM {} WHERE \\"table\\" = ? AND id = ?".format(
                    quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
                ),
                (table, invariant_id),
            )

    def list_import_invariants(self, table: str) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
        """
        Return a list of ``{"id": invariant_id, "expression": sql}``
        dictionaries for the specified table.

        :param table: Name of the table
        """
        self._ensure_import_invariants_table()
        rows = self.execute(
            "SELECT id, expression FROM {} WHERE \\"table\\" = ? ORDER BY rowid".format(
                quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
            ),
            (table,),
        ).fetchall()
        return [{"id": row[0], "expression": row[1]} for row in rows]

    def validate_import_invariants(self, table: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Validate every import invariant registered for ``table``.

        Returns ``{"valid": bool, "failures": [{"id", "expression", "error"}]}``.

        Invariants whose SQL starts with ``SELECT`` are evaluated directly and
        the first column of the first row is treated as truthy/falsy. Other SQL
        is treated as an expression: aggregate expressions are evaluated once
        for the table while non-aggregate expressions must be true for every
        row.
        """
        failures = []
        invariants = self.list_import_invariants(table)
        table_exists = self.table(table).exists()
        count = self.table(table).count if table_exists else 0
        for invariant in invariants:
            expression = invariant["expression"]
            ok = True
            try:
                if expression.lstrip().upper().startswith("SELECT"):
                    rows = self.execute(expression).fetchall()
                    ok = bool(rows and rows[0][0])
                else:
                    rows = self.execute(
                        "SELECT ({}) AS _import_invariant FROM {}".format(
                            expression, quote_identifier(table)
                        )
                    ).fetchall()
                    if count == 0:
                        # Non-aggregate expressions are vacuously true for an
                        # empty table; aggregate expressions return one row.
                        ok = bool(rows and rows[0][0]) if rows else True
                    elif len(rows) == 1:
                        ok = bool(rows[0][0])
                    else:
                        ok = all(bool(row[0]) for row in rows)
            except Exception as e:
                failures.append(
                    {
                        "id": invariant["id"],
                        "expression": expression,
                        "error": str(e),
                    }
                )
                continue
            if not ok:
                failures.append(
                    {
                        "id": invariant["id"],
                        "expression": expression,
                        "error": "expression evaluated to false",
                    }
                )
        return {"valid": not failures, "failures": failures}

    def _format_import_error_report(
        self,
        table: Optional[str],
        error: Optional[Exception],
        failures: List[Dict[str, Any]],
    ) -> str:
        bits = []
        if error is not None:
            bits.append("Import error: {}".format(error))
        if failures:
            bits.append("Import invariant validation failed for table '{}':".format(table))
            for failure in failures:
                bits.append(
                    "  {} {}: {}".format(
                        failure["id"],
                        failure["expression"],
                        failure.get("error") or "false",
                    )
                )
        return "\\n".join(bits)

    def safe_import(
        self,
        table: Optional[str],
        operation: Callable[[], None],
        strict: bool = False,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Run ``operation`` inside a rollback checkpoint. On success the
        checkpoint is committed and ``{"success": True}`` is returned. On
        failure the database is rolled back to its pre-operation state.

        If ``strict`` is ``True`` a failure raises an exception instead of
        returning a result dictionary. Invariant failures raise
        :class:`ImportInvariantError`.
        """
        return self._safe_import_operation(table, operation, strict=strict)

    def _safe_import_operation(
        self,
        table: Optional[str],
        operation: Callable[[], None],
        strict: bool = False,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        old_isolation_level = self.conn.isolation_level
        checkpoint_id = None
        committed = False
        try:
            self.conn.isolation_level = None
            checkpoint_id = self._create_checkpoint()
            error = None
            try:
                operation()
            except Exception as e:
                error = e
            failures: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
            valid = True
            if error is None:
                try:
                    if table is not None:
                        validation = self.validate_import_invariants(table)
                        failures = validation["failures"]
                        valid = validation["valid"]
                except Exception as e:
                    error = e
            if error is None and valid:
                try:
                    self.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                    committed = True
                    return {"success": True}
                except Exception as e:
                    error = e
            if not committed:
                try:
                    self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                except Exception:
                    pass
            if strict:
                if error is not None:
                    raise error
                raise ImportInvariantError(
                    "Import invariant validation failed for table '{}': {}".format(
                        table, failures
                    )
                )
            return {
                "success": False,
                "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                "failures": failures,
                "error_report": self._format_import_error_report(table, error, failures),
            }
        finally:
            self.conn.isolation_level = old_isolation_level

    def safe_bulk_insert(
        self,
        table: str,
        records: Union[Iterable[Dict[str, Any]], Iterable[Sequence[Any]]],
        strict: bool = False,
        pk=DEFAULT,
        batch_size=DEFAULT,
        alter=DEFAULT,
        ignore=DEFAULT,
        replace=DEFAULT,
        truncate: bool = False,
        extracts=DEFAULT,
        conversions=DEFAULT,
        columns=DEFAULT,
        analyze: bool = False,
        hash_id=DEFAULT,
        hash_id_columns=DEFAULT,
        not_null=DEFAULT,
        defaults=DEFAULT,
        column_order=DEFAULT,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Safely insert many records into ``table`` using
        :meth:`Table.insert_all`, with rollback on failure and import
        invariant validation on success.
        """
        return self.safe_import(
            table,
            lambda: self.table(table).insert_all(
                records,
                pk=pk,
                batch_size=batch_size,
                alter=alter,
                ignore=ignore,
                replace=replace,
                truncate=truncate,
                extracts=extracts,
                conversions=conversions,
                columns=columns,
                analyze=analyze,
                hash_id=hash_id,
                hash_id_columns=hash_id_columns,
                not_null=not_null,
                defaults=defaults,
                column_order=column_order,
            ),
            strict=strict,
        )

    def safe_bulk_upsert(
        self,
        table: str,
        records: Union[Iterable[Dict[str, Any]], Iterable[Sequence[Any]]],
        pk,
        strict: bool = False,
        foreign_keys=DEFAULT,
        column_order=DEFAULT,
        not_null=DEFAULT,
        defaults=DEFAULT,
        batch_size=DEFAULT,
        hash_id=DEFAULT,
        hash_id_columns=DEFAULT,
        alter=DEFAULT,
        extracts=DEFAULT,
        conversions=DEFAULT,
        columns=DEFAULT,
        analyze: bool = False,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Safely upsert many records into ``table`` using
        :meth:`Table.upsert_all`, with rollback on failure and import
        invariant validation on success.
        """
        return self.safe_import(
            table,
            lambda: self.table(table).upsert_all(
                records,
                pk=pk,
                foreign_keys=foreign_keys,
                column_order=column_order,
                not_null=not_null,
                defaults=defaults,
                batch_size=batch_size,
                hash_id=hash_id,
                hash_id_columns=hash_id_columns,
                alter=alter,
                extracts=extracts,
                conversions=conversions,
                columns=columns,
                analyze=analyze,
            ),
            strict=strict,
        )

    def safe_bulk_execute(
        self,
        sql: str,
        records: Iterable[Any],
        batch_size: int = 100,
        strict: bool = False,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Safely execute a parameterized SQL statement against ``records`` using
        ``cursor.executemany()``, with rollback on failure.
        """
        return self.safe_import(
            None,
            lambda: self._bulk_execute(sql, records, batch_size),
            strict=strict,
        )

    def _bulk_execute(self, sql: str, records: Iterable[Any], batch_size: int) -> None:
        if batch_size:
            doc_chunks = chunks(records, batch_size)
        else:
            doc_chunks = [records]
        for doc_chunk in doc_chunks:
            self.conn.cursor().executemany(sql, doc_chunk)

    def import_csv(
        self,
        table: str,
        source,
        safe_mode: bool = False,
        strict: bool = False,
        pk=DEFAULT,
        batch_size=DEFAULT,
        alter=DEFAULT,
        delimiter: Optional[str] = None,
        quotechar: Optional[str] = None,
        no_headers: bool = False,
        encoding: str = "utf-8-sig",
        empty_null: bool = False,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        """
        Import records from a CSV file into ``table``.

        ``source`` may be a path string or a text file-like object. When
        ``safe_mode=True`` the import is atomic and validates import
        invariants.
        """
        close = False
        if isinstance(source, (str, pathlib.Path)):
            file = open(source, "r", encoding=encoding, newline="")
            close = True
        else:
            file = source
        try:
            reader = csv_module.reader(
                file,
                delimiter=delimiter or ",",
                quotechar=quotechar or '"',
            )
            first_row = next(reader)
            if no_headers:
                headers = ["untitled_{}".format(i + 1) for i in range(len(first_row))]
                reader = itertools.chain([first_row], reader)
            else:
                headers = first_row
            if empty_null:
                records = (
                    dict(
                        zip(
                            headers,
                            [None if cell == "" else cell for cell in row],
                        )
                    )
                    for row in reader
                )
            else:
                records = (dict(zip(headers, row)) for row in reader)
            if safe_mode:
                return self.safe_bulk_insert(
                    table,
                    records,
                    strict=strict,
                    pk=pk,
                    batch_size=batch_size,
                    alter=alter,
                    **kwargs,
                )
            return self.table(table).insert_all(
                records,
                pk=pk,
                batch_size=batch_size,
                alter=alter,
                **kwargs,
            )
        finally:
            if close:
                file.close()

    def import_json(
        self,
        table: str,
        data,
        safe_mode: bool = False,
        strict: bool = False,
        pk=DEFAULT,
        batch_size=DEFAULT,
        alter=DEFAULT,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        """
        Import records from a JSON list (or a JSON string) into ``table``.

        When ``safe_mode=True`` the import is atomic and validates import
        invariants.
        """
        if isinstance(data, str):
            data = json.loads(data)
        if isinstance(data, dict):
            data = [data]
        if safe_mode:
            return self.safe_bulk_insert(
                table,
                data,
                strict=strict,
                pk=pk,
                batch_size=batch_size,
                alter=alter,
                **kwargs,
            )
        return self.table(table).insert_all(
            data,
            pk=pk,
            batch_size=batch_size,
            alter=alter,
            **kwargs,
        )

import io, json
from sqlite_utils import Database

def t(name, fn):
    try:
        fn()
        print("PASS:", name)
    except Exception as e:
        print("FAIL:", name, "->", type(e).__name__, e)
        import traceback; traceback.print_exc()

# --- Checkpoint basics ---
def cp_basic():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "one"}, pk="id")
    db.enable_safe_import()
    cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    assert isinstance(cid, str) and cid
    db["t"].insert({"id": 2, "name": "two"}, pk="id")
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
    assert db["t"].count == 1, db["t"].count
    try:
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotActiveError", e
    try:
        db.commit_checkpoint("nope")
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotFoundError", e
    # cleanup finalized
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(cid)
    try:
        db.commit_checkpoint(cid)
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotFoundError", e
t("checkpoint basics", cp_basic)

def cp_commit():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "one"}, pk="id")
    db.commit_checkpoint(cid)
    assert db["t"].count == 1
    try:
        db.commit_checkpoint(cid)
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotActiveError"
t("checkpoint commit", cp_commit)

def cp_nested():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    cid1 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    cid2 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 2}, pk="id")
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid2)
    assert db["t"].count == 1
    db.commit_checkpoint(cid1)
    assert db["t"].count == 1
t("nested checkpoints", cp_nested)

def cp_cleanup_active():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(cid)
    assert db["t"].count == 1  # cleanup releases (keeps writes)
    try:
        db.commit_checkpoint(cid)
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotFoundError"
t("cleanup active", cp_cleanup_active)

# --- Invariants ---
def inv_basic():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")
    iid = db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) = 2")
    assert isinstance(iid, str) and iid
    result = db.validate_import_invariants("items")
    assert result == {"valid": True, "failures": []}, result
    listed = db.list_import_invariants("items")
    assert listed == [{"id": iid, "expression": "COUNT(*) = 2"}], listed
    db.remove_import_invariant("items", iid)
    assert db.list_import_invariants("items") == []
t("invariant basic", inv_basic)

def inv_fail():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "a"}], pk="id")
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 1")
    result = db.validate_import_invariants("items")
    assert result["valid"] is False
    assert len(result["failures"]) == 1
    assert result["failures"][0]["id"]
    assert result["failures"][0]["expression"] == "COUNT(*) > 1"
t("invariant fail", inv_fail)

def inv_rowwise():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "id > 0")
    assert db.validate_import_invariants("items")["valid"] is True
    db["items"].insert({"id": -1, "name": "neg"}, pk="id")
    result = db.validate_import_invariants("items")
    assert result["valid"] is False
    # select-style invariant
    db2 = Database(memory=True)
    db2["x"].insert({"a": 1})
    db2.add_import_invariant("x", "SELECT a = 1 FROM x")
    assert db2.validate_import_invariants("x")["valid"] is True
    db2["x"].insert({"a": 0})
    assert db2.validate_import_invariants("x")["valid"] is False
t("invariant rowwise + select", inv_rowwise)

# --- Safe operations ---
def safe_success():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 0")
    result = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")
    assert result == {"success": True}, result
    assert db["items"].count == 2
t("safe bulk insert success", safe_success)

def safe_insert_fail_rollback():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    result = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [
        {"id": 2, "name": "b"},
        {"id": 3, "name": "c"},
        {"id": 1, "name": "dup"},
    ], pk="id", batch_size=1)
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert result["checkpoint_id"]
    assert "UNIQUE constraint failed" in result["error_report"] or "Import error" in result["error_report"]
    assert db["items"].count == 1, db["items"].count  # no partial insert of id 2,3
    assert [r["id"] for r in db["items"].rows] == [1]
t("safe insert fail rollback", safe_insert_fail_rollback)

def safe_invariant_fail_rollback():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    result = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert len(result["failures"]) == 1
    assert "invariant" in result["error_report"].lower() or "valid" in result["error_report"].lower()
    assert db.table_names() == [] or "items" not in db.table_names(), db.table_names()
t("safe invariant fail rollback", safe_invariant_fail_rollback)

def safe_strict_raise():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    try:
        db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id", strict=True)
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        msg = str(e).lower()
        assert "invariant" in msg or "valid" in msg or "validation" in msg, msg
    # operation error raises original
    db2 = Database(memory=True)
    db2["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    try:
        db2.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 2}, {"id": 1}], pk="id", batch_size=1, strict=True)
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert "UNIQUE" in str(e), str(e)
t("safe strict raise", safe_strict_raise)

def safe_schema_rollback():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["existing"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    db.create_index("existing", ["name"], unique=True)
    db.execute("CREATE TRIGGER existing_trig AFTER INSERT ON existing BEGIN SELECT 1; END")
    before_schema = db.schema
    before_tables = db.table_names()
    before_triggers = db.triggers_dict
    result = db.safe_bulk_insert(
        "new_table",
        [{"id": 1, "name": "x"}, {"id": 2, "name": "x"}],
        pk="id",
        batch_size=1,
    )
    # duplicate pk? no - new table no duplicate. Make it fail via invariant
    db2 = Database(memory=True)
    db2.enable_safe_import()
    db2.add_import_invariant("new_table", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    result2 = db2.safe_bulk_insert("new_table", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id")
    assert result2["success"] is False
    assert "new_table" not in db2.table_names(), db2.table_names()
    # index/trigger rollback
    db3 = Database(memory=True)
    db3["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    db3.create_index("t", ["name"])
    db3.execute("CREATE TRIGGER trg AFTER INSERT ON t BEGIN SELECT 1; END")
    before_schema3 = db3.schema
    db3.enable_safe_import()
    db3.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    result3 = db3.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")
    assert result3["success"] is False
    assert db3.schema == before_schema3, (before_schema3, db3.schema)
    assert db3.table_names() == ["t"]
    assert set(db3.triggers_dict.keys()) == {"trg"}
    assert db3["t"].indexes[0].name  # index still there
t("safe schema rollback", safe_schema_rollback)

def safe_upsert():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) < 3")
    r = db.safe_bulk_upsert("t", [{"id": 1, "name": "b"}, {"id": 2, "name": "c"}], pk="id")
    assert r == {"success": True}, r
    assert db["t"].count == 2
    db2 = Database(memory=True)
    db2["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    db2.enable_safe_import()
    db2.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    r2 = db2.safe_bulk_upsert("t", [{"id": 1, "name": "b"}, {"id": 2, "name": "c"}], pk="id")
    assert r2["success"] is False
    assert db2["t"].count == 1
    assert db2["t"].get(1)["name"] == "a"
t("safe upsert", safe_upsert)

def import_csv_json():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 0")
    csv_text = "id,name\n1,a\n2,b\n"
    r = db.import_csv("items", io.StringIO(csv_text), safe_mode=True, pk="id")
    assert r == {"success": True}, r
    assert db["items"].count == 2
    r2 = db.import_json("items", [{"id": 3, "name": "c"}], safe_mode=True, pk="id")
    assert r2 == {"success": True}
    assert db["items"].count == 3
    # failure via invariant
    db2 = Database(memory=True)
    db2.enable_safe_import()
    db2.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 1")
    r3 = db2.import_csv("items", io.StringIO("id,name\n1,a\n"), safe_mode=True, pk="id")
    assert r3["success"] is False
    assert "items" not in db2.table_names()
t("import csv/json", import_csv_json)

def safe_bulk_execute_update():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")

t("safe bulk execute update", safe_bulk_execute_update)

'''

    def _transaction(self) -> Generator[None, None, None]:
        """
        Context manager for internal transactions. During a safe import the
        context manager does nothing, so that ``with self.conn:`` blocks do not
        commit the active rollback checkpoint. Otherwise it behaves like
        ``with self.conn:``.
        """
        if getattr(self, "_safe_import_in_progress", False):
            yield
        else:
            with self.conn:
                yield

   435	
   436	    @contextlib.contextmanager
   437	    def ensure_autocommit_off(self) -> Generator[None, None, None]:
   438	        """
   439	        Ensure autocommit is off for this database connection.
   440	
   441	        Example usage::
   442	
   443	            with db.ensure_autocommit_off():
   444	                # do stuff here
   445	
   446	        This will reset to the previous autocommit state at the end of the block.

   459	        context manager does nothing, so that ``with self.conn:`` blocks do not
   460	        commit the active rollback checkpoint. Otherwise it behaves like
   461	        ``with self.conn:``.
   462	        """
   463	        if getattr(self, "_safe_import_in_progress", False):
   464	            yield
   465	        else:
   466	            with self._transaction():
   467	                yield
   468	
   469	        self, tracer: Optional[Callable[[str, Optional[Sequence]], None]] = None
   470	    ) -> Generator["Database", None, None]:
   471	        """
   472	        Context manager to temporarily set a tracer function - all executed SQL queries will
   473	        be passed to this.
   474	
   475	        The tracer function should accept two arguments: ``sql`` and ``parameters``
   476	
   477	        Example usage::
   478	
   479	            with db.tracer(print):
   480	                db["creatures"].insert({"name": "Cleo"})
   481	
   482	        See :ref:`python_api_tracing`.
   483	
   484	        :param tracer: Callable accepting ``sql`` and ``parameters`` arguments
   485	        """
   486	        prev_tracer = self._tracer
   487	        self._tracer = tracer or print
   488	        try:
   489	            yield self
   490	        finally:

END STEP

        """
        Context manager for internal transactions. During a safe import the
        context manager does nothing, so that ``with self.conn:`` blocks do not
        commit the active rollback checkpoint. Otherwise it behaves like
        ``with self.conn:``.

  1283	    Insert records from FILE into a table, creating the table if it
  1284	    does not already exist.
  1285	
  1286	    Example:
  1287	
  1288	        echo '{"name": "Lila"}' | sqlite-utils insert data.db chickens -
  1289	
  1290	    By default the input is expected to be a JSON object or array of objects.
  1291	
  1292	    \b
  1293	    - Use --nl for newline-delimited JSON objects
  1294	    - Use --csv or --tsv for comma-separated or tab-separated input
  1295	    - Use --lines to write each incoming line to a column called "line"
  1296	    - Use --text to write the entire input to a column called "text"
  1297	
  1298	    You can also use --convert to pass a fragment of Python code that will
  1299	    be used to convert each input.
  1300	
  1301	    Your Python code will be passed a "row" variable representing the
  1302	    imported row, and can return a modified row.
  1303	
  1304	    This example uses just the name, latitude and longitude columns from
  1305	    a CSV file, converting name to upper case and latitude and longitude
  1306	    to floating point numbers:
  1307	
  1308	    \b
  1309	        sqlite-utils insert plants.db plants plants.csv --csv --convert '
  1310	          return {
  1311	            "name": row["name"].upper(),
  1312	            "latitude": float(row["latitude"]),
  1313	            "longitude": float(row["longitude"]),
  1314	          }'
  1315	
  1316	    If you are using --lines your code will be passed a "line" variable,
  1317	    and for --text a "text" variable.
  1318	
  1319	    When using --text your function can return an iterator of rows to
  1320	    insert. This example inserts one record per word in the input:
  1321	
  1322	    \b
  1323	        echo 'A bunch of words' | sqlite-utils insert words.db words - \\
  1324	          --text --convert '({"word": w} for w in text.split())'

path = 'sqlite_utils/cli.py'
src = open(path).read()

anchor = '\n\n@cli.command(name="create-database")\n'
block = '''


@cli.command(name="enable-safe-import")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@load_extension_option
def enable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
    """Enable safe import mode for a database

    While enabled, ``create_import_checkpoint()`` can be used to create
    rollback checkpoints and bulk imports can be run atomically.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils enable-safe-import data.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.enable_safe_import()


@cli.command(name="disable-safe-import")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@load_extension_option
def disable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
    """Disable safe import mode for a database

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils disable-safe-import data.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.disable_safe_import()


@cli.command(name="add-import-invariant")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@click.argument("sql")
@load_extension_option
def add_import_invariant(path, table, sql, load_extension):
    """Add an import invariant to a table

    The invariant is an SQL expression, or a SELECT statement. It is
    validated after every safe-mode import.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db items 'COUNT(*) > 0'
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant(table, sql)
    click.echo(invariant_id)


@cli.command(name="remove-import-invariant")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@click.argument("invariant_id")
@load_extension_option
def remove_import_invariant(path, table, invariant_id, load_extension):
    """Remove an import invariant from a table

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db items inv_abc123
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.remove_import_invariant(table, invariant_id)


@cli.command(name="list-import-invariants")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@load_extension_option
def list_import_invariants(path, table, load_extension):
    """List import invariants for a table

    Outputs each invariant id followed by its SQL.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils list-import-invariants data.db items
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    for invariant in db.list_import_invariants(table):
        click.echo("{}\\t{}".format(invariant["id"], invariant["expression"]))


@cli.command(name="validate-import-invariants")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@load_extension_option
def validate_import_invariants(path, table, load_extension):
    """Validate import invariants for a table

    Always exits 0, even if invariants fail. Output indicates pass or fail
    and lists any failing invariant ids.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db items
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    result = db.validate_import_invariants(table)
    if result["valid"]:
        click.echo("Pass: all import invariants for '{}' are valid".format(table))
    else:
        click.echo("Fail: {} import invariant(s) failed for '{}'".format(len(result["failures"]), table))
        for failure in result["failures"]:
            click.echo("  {}".format(failure["id"]))
'''
assert anchor in src
src = src.replace(anchor, block + anchor, 1)
open(path, 'w').write(src)
print("done")

cd /app && cat > /tmp/test_cli_safe.py <<'EOF'
from click.testing import CliRunner
from sqlite_utils import cli, Database
import json, io, pathlib, tempfile, os

runner = CliRunner()
tmp = tempfile.mkdtemp()
db_path = os.path.join(tmp, "data.db")

def run(args, **kw):
    return runner.invoke(cli.cli, args, **kw)

# Setup
db = Database(db_path)
db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")

# enable-safe-import / add invariant
r = run(["enable-safe-import", db_path])
assert r.exit_code == 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)
r = run(["add-import-invariant", db_path, "items", "COUNT(*) < 3"])
assert r.exit_code == 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)
iid = r.output.strip()
assert iid
r = run(["list-import-invariants", db_path, "items"])
assert r.exit_code == 0
assert iid in r.output and "COUNT(*) < 3" in r.output, r.output
r = run(["validate-import-invariants", db_path, "items"])
assert r.exit_code == 0
assert "Pass" in r.output, r.output

# safe insert that violates invariant -> non-zero, no changes
r = run(["insert", db_path, "items", "-", "--pk", "id", "--safe-mode"], input=json.dumps({"id": 3, "name": "c"}))
assert r.exit_code != 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)
assert "invariant" in r.output.lower() or "valid" in r.output.lower(), r.output
assert Database(db_path)["items"].count == 2

# safe insert success
r = run(["insert", db_path, "items2", "-", "--pk", "id", "--safe-mode"], input=json.dumps({"id": 1, "name": "x"}))
assert r.exit_code == 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)
assert Database(db_path)["items2"].count == 1

# safe upsert
r = run(["upsert", db_path, "items", "-", "--pk", "id", "--safe-mode"], input=json.dumps({"id": 1, "name": "updated"}))
assert r.exit_code == 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)
assert Database(db_path)["items"].get(1)["name"] == "updated"

# safe bulk with UPDATE
r = run(["bulk", db_path, "update items set name = :name where id = :id", "-", "--nl", "--safe-mode"], input='{"id": 1, "name": "bulk_updated"}\n')
assert r.exit_code == 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)
assert Database(db_path)["items"].get(1)["name"] == "bulk_updated"

# safe bulk failure
r = run(["bulk", db_path, "insert into items (id, name) values (:id, :name)", "-", "--nl", "--safe-mode"], input='{"id": 1, "name": "dup"}\n')
assert r.exit_code != 0, (r.exit_code, r.output)

# remove invariant
r = run(["remove-import-invariant", db_path, "items", iid])
assert r.exit_code == 0
r = run(["validate-import-invariants", db_path, "items"])
assert r.exit_code == 0 and "Pass" in r.output

# disable
r = run(["disable-safe-import", db_path])
assert r.exit_code == 0

print("ALL CLI TESTS PASSED")
EOF

ALL CLI TESTS PASSED

.. _cli_spatialite_indexes:

Adding spatial indexes
----------------------

Once you have a geometry column, you can speed up bounding box queries by adding a spatial index:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils create-spatial-index spatial.db locations geometry

See this `SpatiaLite Cookbook recipe <http://www.gaia-gis.it/gaia-sins/spatialite-cookbook-5/cookbook_topics.03.html#topic_Wonderful_RTree_Spatial_Index>`__ for examples of how to use a spatial index.

.. _cli_install:

Installing packages
===================

The :ref:`convert command <cli_convert>` and the :ref:`insert -\\-convert <cli_insert_convert>` and :ref:`query -\\-functions <cli_query_functions>` options can be provided with a Python script that imports additional modules from the ``sqlite-utils`` environment.

You can install packages from PyPI directly into the correct environment using ``sqlite-utils install <package>``. This is a wrapper around ``pip install``.

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils install beautifulsoup4

Use ``-U`` to upgrade an existing package.

.. _cli_uninstall:

Uninstalling packages
=====================

You can uninstall packages that were installed using ``sqlite-utils install`` with ``sqlite-utils uninstall <package>``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils uninstall beautifulsoup4

Use ``-y`` to skip the request for confirmation.

END STEP

block = '''.. _cli_safe_import:\n\nSafe imports and import invariants\n==================================\n\nBulk imports can fail part-way through, leaving a database in an inconsistent state. ``sqlite-utils`` provides a safe import mode that wraps an import in a rollback checkpoint, validates any configured import invariants after the writes and only commits if everything succeeds.\n\nUse the ``--safe-mode`` option with ``insert``, ``upsert`` and ``bulk`` to run the operation in safe mode. If the operation succeeds the changes are committed and the command exits ``0``. If it fails (either because of a SQL error or because an import invariant is not satisfied) the database is rolled back to its exact pre-operation state - including any schema changes such as tables, columns, indexes and triggers - and the command exits with a non-zero code.\n\n.. code-block:: bash\n\n    sqlite-utils insert data.db items items.csv --csv --safe-mode\n\nYou can register persistent import invariants for a table using ``add-import-invariant``. The invariant is an SQL expression or a ``SELECT`` statement that must hold after every safe-mode import. For example, to require that the ``items`` table never ends up empty:\n\n.. code-block:: bash\n\n    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db items 'COUNT(*) > 0'\n\nInvariants are stored inside the database itself. Use ``list-import-invariants`` to see them, ``remove-import-invariant`` to delete one and ``validate-import-invariants`` to check them against the current contents of the table:\n\n.. code-block:: bash\n\n    sqlite-utils list-import-invariants data.db items\n    sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db items inv_abc123\n    sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db items\n\nThe ``validate-import-invariants`` command always exits ``0`` - its output indicates whether the invariants passed or failed and lists any failing invariant ids.\n\nSafe import mode can be enabled and disabled for a database using:\n\n.. code-block:: bash\n\n    sqlite-utils enable-safe-import data.db\n    sqlite-utils disable-safe-import data.db\n\nWhen safe import mode is enabled the Python API's ``create_import_checkpoint()``, ``commit_checkpoint()``, ``rollback_to_checkpoint()`` and ``cleanup_checkpoint()`` methods can be used to manage rollback checkpoints directly.\n\n'''
assert anchor in src
src = src.replace(anchor, block + anchor, 1)

old = """    sqlite-utils insert data.db chickens -

.. code-block:: bash

You can skip inserting any records that have a primary key that already exists using ``ignore=True``. This works with both ``.insert({...}, ignore=True)`` and ``.insert_all([...], ignore=True)``.

You can delete all the existing rows in the table before inserting the new records using ``truncate=True``. This is useful if you want to replace the data in the table.

Pass ``analyze=True`` to run ``ANALYZE`` against the table after inserting the new records.

.. _python_api_insert_lists:

Inserting data from a list or tuple iterator
--------------------------------------------

As an alternative to passing an iterator of dictionaries, you can pass an iterator of lists or tuples. The first item yielded by the iterator must be a list or tuple of string column names, and subsequent items should be lists or tuples of values:

.. code-block:: python

    db["creatures"].insert_all([
        ["name", "species"],
        ["Cleo", "dog"],

path = 'docs/python-api.rst'
src = open(path).read()

anchor = '.. _python_api_insert_lists:\n'
block = '''.. _python_api_safe_imports:\n\nSafe imports and import invariants\n----------------------------------\n\nBulk imports can fail part-way through, leaving a database in an inconsistent state. ``sqlite-utils`` provides safe import methods that wrap the import in a rollback checkpoint, validate any configured import invariants after the writes, and only commit if everything succeeds.\n\nThe ``safe_bulk_insert()`` and ``safe_bulk_upsert()`` methods on :class:`Database <sqlite_utils.db.Database>` work like ``insert_all()`` and ``upsert_all()`` but are atomic:\n\n.. code-block:: python\n\n    db = Database(\"data.db\")\n    result = db.safe_bulk_insert(\n        \"items\",\n        [{\"id\": 1, \"name\": \"Cleo\"}, {\"id\": 2, \"name\": \"Marnie\"}],\n        pk=\"id\",\n    )\n\nWith ``strict=False`` (the default) these methods return ``{\"success\": True}`` on success, or a dictionary containing ``success: False``, a ``checkpoint_id``, ``failures`` and an ``error_report`` on failure. With ``strict=True`` they roll back and then raise an exception.\n\nYou can also import from CSV and JSON files atomically:\n\n.. code-block:: python\n\n    db.import_csv(\"items\", \"items.csv\", safe_mode=True, pk=\"id\")\n    db.import_json(\"items\", [{\"id\": 1, \"name\": \"Cleo\"}], safe_mode=True, pk=\"id\")\n\nImport invariants are persistent SQL expressions that must be satisfied after every safe-mode import. Register them using ``add_import_invariant()`` and validate them with ``validate_import_invariants()``:\n\n.. code-block:: python\n\n    db.add_import_invariant(\"items\", \"COUNT(*) > 0\")\n    db.validate_import_invariants(\"items\")\n    # {\"valid\": True, \"failures\": []}\n\nInvariants whose SQL starts with ``SELECT`` are evaluated directly and the first column of the first row is treated as truthy/falsy. Other SQL is treated as an expression: aggregate expressions such as ``COUNT(*)``, ``SUM(...)`` and ``AVG(...)`` are evaluated once for the table, while non-aggregate expressions must be true for every row.\n\nUse ``list_import_invariants()`` to see the invariants for a table and ``remove_import_invariant()`` to remove one.\n\nYou can also manage rollback checkpoints directly. Enable safe import mode first:\n\n.. code-block:: python\n\n    db.enable_safe_import()\n    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()\n    # ... perform writes ...\n    db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)  # keep the changes\n    # or: db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)  # discard the changes\n\n``create_import_checkpoint()`` raises ``SafeImportNotEnabledError`` if safe import mode is not enabled. Committing or rolling back a checkpoint finalizes it; further operations on the same id raise ``CheckpointNotActiveError``. Unknown or cleaned up ids raise ``CheckpointNotFoundError``. Nested checkpoints are supported, and ``cleanup_checkpoint()`` removes an id from tracking.\n\n'''
assert anchor in src
src = src.replace(anchor, block + anchor, 1)
open(path, 'w').write(src)
print("done")

from sqlite_utils import Database

def t(name, fn):
    try:
        fn(); print("PASS:", name)
    except Exception as e:
        print("FAIL:", name, "->", type(e).__name__, e)

def nested_commit_then_outer_rollback():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    c1 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    c2 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 2}, pk="id")
    db.commit_checkpoint(c2)
    assert db["t"].count == 2
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(c1)
    assert db["t"].count == 0, db["t"].count
t("nested commit then outer rollback", nested_commit_then_outer_rollback)

def cleanup_finalized():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    c1 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    db.commit_checkpoint(c1)
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(c1)
    try:
        db.commit_checkpoint(c1); assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotFoundError"
t("cleanup finalized", cleanup_finalized)

def cleanup_unknown():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    try:
        db.cleanup_checkpoint("nope"); assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert type(e).__name__ == "CheckpointNotFoundError"
t("cleanup unknown", cleanup_unknown)

def import_csv_path():
    import tempfile, os
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 0")
    path = os.path.join(tempfile.mkdtemp(), "items.csv")
    open(path, "w").write("id,name\n1,a\n2,b\n")
    r = db.import_csv("items", path, safe_mode=True, pk="id")
    assert r == {"success": True}, r
    assert db["items"].count == 2
t("import_csv path", import_csv_path)

def import_json_string():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    r = db.import_json("items", '[{"id": 1, "name": "a"}]', safe_mode=True, pk="id")
    assert r == {"success": True}, r
    assert db["items"].count == 1
t("import_json string", import_json_string)

def strict_csv():
    import io
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 1")
    try:
        db.import_csv("items", io.StringIO("id,name\n1,a\n"), safe_mode=True, strict=True, pk="id")
        assert False
    except Exception as e:
        assert "invariant" in str(e).lower() or "valid" in str(e).lower(), str(e)
    assert "items" not in db.table_names()
t("strict csv invariant raise", strict_csv)

def safe_import_no_autocommit_restore():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    old = db.conn.isolation_level
    db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}], pk="id")
    assert db.conn.isolation_level == old
    # safe import enabled flag unchanged
    assert db._safe_import_enabled is False
    assert db._safe_import_in_progress is False
t("safe op restores state", safe_import_no_autocommit_restore)

def enable_disable_restores():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    old = db.conn.isolation_level
    db.enable_safe_import()
    assert db.conn.isolation_level is None
    assert db._safe_import_in_progress is True
    db.disable_safe_import()
    assert db.conn.isolation_level == old
    assert db._safe_import_in_progress is False
t("enable/disable restores", enable_disable_restores)

cd /app && cat > tests/test_safe_import.py <<'EOF'
import io
import json
from click.testing import CliRunner
import pytest
from sqlite_utils import Database, cli
from sqlite_utils.db import (
    CheckpointNotActiveError,
    CheckpointNotFoundError,
    ImportInvariantError,
    SafeImportNotEnabledError,
)


@pytest.fixture
def safe_db():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    return db


def test_create_checkpoint_requires_safe_import():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    with pytest.raises(SafeImportNotEnabledError):
        db.create_import_checkpoint()


def test_checkpoint_commit_and_rollback_finalize():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    assert checkpoint_id
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    assert db["t"].count == 1
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)


def test_checkpoint_rollback_restores_data_and_schema():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    db["t"].create_index(["name"])
    db.execute("CREATE TRIGGER trg AFTER INSERT ON t BEGIN SELECT 1; END")
    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 2, "name": "b"}, pk="id")
    db["t"].add_column("extra", str)
    db.execute("CREATE INDEX idx2 ON t (extra)")
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    assert db["t"].count == 1
    assert [c.name for c in db["t"].columns] == ["id", "name"]
    assert [i.name for i in db["t"].indexes] == [db["t"].indexes[0].name]
    assert set(db.triggers_dict.keys()) == {"trg"}


def test_checkpoint_unknown_and_cleanup():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.cleanup_checkpoint("missing")


def test_nested_checkpoints():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    outer = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    inner = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 2}, pk="id")
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(inner)
    assert db["t"].count == 1
    db.commit_checkpoint(outer)
    assert db["t"].count == 1


def test_import_invariant_lifecycle():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id")
    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) = 2")
    assert invariant_id
    assert db.list_import_invariants("items") == [
        {"id": invariant_id, "expression": "COUNT(*) = 2"}
    ]
    assert db.validate_import_invariants("items") == {"valid": True, "failures": []}
    db.remove_import_invariant("items", invariant_id)
    assert db.list_import_invariants("items") == []


def test_import_invariant_failures():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id")
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 2")
    result = db.validate_import_invariants("items")
    assert result["valid"] is False
    assert len(result["failures"]) == 1
    assert set(result["failures"][0].keys()) == {"id", "expression", "error"}


def test_import_invariant_aggregate_and_rowwise():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert_all([{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id")
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) = 2")
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "id > 0")
    assert db.validate_import_invariants("items")["valid"] is True
    db["items"].insert({"id": -1}, pk="id")
    result = db.validate_import_invariants("items")
    assert result["valid"] is False
    assert len(result["failures"]) == 1


def test_import_invariant_select():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "SELECT id = 1 FROM items")
    assert db.validate_import_invariants("items")["valid"] is True


def test_safe_bulk_insert_success(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 0")
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_insert(
        "items",
        [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}],
        pk="id",
    )
    assert result == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["items"].count == 2


def test_safe_bulk_insert_rolls_back_on_error():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["items"].insert({"id": 1}, pk="id")
    result = db.safe_bulk_insert(
        "items",
        [{"id": 2}, {"id": 3}, {"id": 1}],
        pk="id",
        batch_size=1,
    )
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert result["checkpoint_id"]
    assert result["failures"] == []
    assert "Import error" in result["error_report"]
    assert db["items"].count == 1


def test_safe_bulk_insert_rolls_back_on_invariant(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_insert(
        "items", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id"
    )
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert len(result["failures"]) == 1
    assert "invariant" in result["error_report"].lower()
    assert "items" not in safe_db.table_names()


def test_safe_bulk_insert_strict_raises(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 2")
    with pytest.raises(ImportInvariantError) as excinfo:
        safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], pk="id", strict=True)
    message = str(excinfo.value).lower()
    assert "valid" in message or "validation" in message or "invariant" in message


def test_safe_bulk_upsert(safe_db):
    safe_db["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) < 3")
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_upsert(
        "t", [{"id": 1, "name": "b"}, {"id": 2, "name": "c"}], pk="id"
    )
    assert result == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["t"].get(1)["name"] == "b"
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 2


def test_safe_bulk_execute_update(safe_db):
    safe_db["t"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}], pk="id")
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_execute(
        "UPDATE t SET name = :name WHERE id = :id",
        [{"id": 1, "name": "x"}, {"id": 2, "name": "y"}],
    )
    assert result == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["t"].get(1)["name"] == "x"
    assert safe_db["t"].get(2)["name"] == "y"


def test_import_csv(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 0")
    result = safe_db.import_csv(
        "items", io.StringIO("id,name\n1,a\n2,b\n"), safe_mode=True, pk="id"
    )
    assert result == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["items"].count == 2


def test_import_json(safe_db):
    result = safe_db.import_json(
        "items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}], safe_mode=True, pk="id"
    )
    assert result == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["items"].count == 1


def test_import_json_string(safe_db):
    result = safe_db.import_json(
        "items", '[{"id": 1, "name": "a"}]', safe_mode=True, pk="id"
    )
    assert result == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["items"].count == 1


@pytest.fixture
def cli_db_path(tmpdir):
    from sqlite_utils.utils import sqlite3
    path = str(tmpdir / "data.db")
    db = sqlite3.connect(path)
    db.execute("create table items (id integer primary key, name text)")
    db.commit()
    db.close()
    return path


def test_cli_safe_insert_success(cli_db_path):
    result = CliRunner().invoke(
        cli.cli,
        ["insert", cli_db_path, "items", "-", "--pk", "id", "--safe-mode"],
        input=json.dumps({"id": 1, "name": "a"}),
    )
    assert result.exit_code == 0, result.output
    assert Database(cli_db_path)["items"].count == 1


def test_cli_safe_insert_failure(cli_db_path):
    db = Database(cli_db_path)
    db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) < 1")
    result = CliRunner().invoke(
        cli.cli,
        ["insert", cli_db_path, "items", "-", "--pk", "id", "--safe-mode"],
        input=json.dumps({"id": 1, "name": "a"}),
    )
    assert result.exit_code != 0
    assert Database(cli_db_path)["items"].count == 0


def test_cli_safe_bulk_update(cli_db_path):
    db = Database(cli_db_path)
    db["items"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "a"}, pk="id")
    result = CliRunner().invoke(
        cli.cli,
        [
            "bulk",
            cli_db_path,
            "update items set name = :name where id = :id",
            "-",
            "--nl",
            "--safe-mode",
        ],
        input='{"id": 1, "name": "updated"}\n',
    )
    assert result.exit_code == 0, result.output
    assert Database(cli_db_path)["items"].get(1)["name"] == "updated"


def test_cli_import_invariant_commands(cli_db_path):
    runner = CliRunner()
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["add-import-invariant", cli_db_path, "items", "COUNT(*) >= 0"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    invariant_id = result.output.strip()
    assert invariant_id
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["list-import-invariants", cli_db_path, "items"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    assert invariant_id in result.output
    assert "COUNT(*) >= 0" in result.output
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["validate-import-invariants", cli_db_path, "items"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    assert "Pass" in result.output
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["remove-import-invariant", cli_db_path, "items", invariant_id])
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["list-import-invariants", cli_db_path, "items"])
    assert invariant_id not in result.output

   892	    def enable_safe_import(self) -> None:
   893	        """
   894	        Enable safe import mode for this database. While enabled the
   895	        connection runs in autocommit mode and :meth:`create_import_checkpoint`
   896	        can be used to create rollback checkpoints.
   897	        """
   898	        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
   899	            self._safe_import_previous_isolation_level = self.conn.isolation_level
   900	            self.conn.isolation_level = None
   901	            self._safe_import_enabled = True
   902	            self._safe_import_in_progress = True
   903	
   904	    def disable_safe_import(self) -> None:
   905	        """
   906	        Disable safe import mode for this database, restoring the previous
   907	        transaction behaviour.
   908	        """
   909	        if self._safe_import_enabled:
   910	            self.conn.isolation_level = self._safe_import_previous_isolation_level
   911	            self._safe_import_enabled = False
   912	            self._safe_import_in_progress = False
   913	
   914	    def _create_checkpoint(self) -> str:
   915	        checkpoint_id = "sp_" + secrets.token_hex(16)
   916	        self.execute(
   917	            "SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id))
   918	        )
   919	        self._active_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
   920	        return checkpoint_id
   921	
   922	    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
   923	        """
   924	        Create a rollback checkpoint and return its checkpoint id. The id is
   925	        an opaque non-empty string.
   926	
   927	        Raises :class:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode is not
   928	        currently enabled.
   929	        """
   930	        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
   931	            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError(
   932	                "Safe import mode is not enabled - call enable_safe_import() first"
   933	            )
   934	        return self._create_checkpoint()
   935	
   936	    def _require_active_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
   937	        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
   938	            return
   939	        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
   940	            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(
   941	                "Checkpoint {} is no longer active".format(checkpoint_id)
   942	            )
   943	        raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
   944	            "Checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
   945	        )
   946	
   947	    def commit_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
   948	        """
   949	        Commit a checkpoint created using :meth:`create_import_checkpoint`.
   950	        Any changes made since the checkpoint was created are kept.
   951	
   952	        Committing finalizes the checkpoint: further commit or rollback calls
   953	        with the same id will raise :class:`CheckpointNotActiveError`.
   954	        """
   955	        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
   956	        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
   957	        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
   958	        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
   959	
   960	    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
   961	        """
   962	        Roll back to a checkpoint created using :meth:`create_import_checkpoint`,
   963	        restoring the exact database state (including schema changes) from when
   964	        the checkpoint was created.
   965	
   966	        Rolling back finalizes the checkpoint: further commit or rollback calls
   967	        with the same id will raise :class:`CheckpointNotActiveError`.
   968	        """
   969	        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
   970	        quoted = quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)
   971	        self.execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
   972	        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
   973	        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
   974	        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
   975	
   976	    def cleanup_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
   977	        """
   978	        Remove a checkpoint id from tracking. Active checkpoints are released
   979	        (their changes are kept) and finalized checkpoints are simply forgotten.
   980	
   981	        After cleanup, commit/rollback calls with the same id raise
   982	        :class:`Checkpoi

  1044	        :param table: Name of the table

  1048	            'SELECT id, expression FROM {} WHERE "table" = ? ORDER BY rowid'.format(

  1055	    def validate_import_invariants(self, table: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
  1056	        """
  1057	        Validate every import invariant registered for ``table``.
  1058	
  1059	        Returns ``{"valid": bool, "failures": [{"id", "expression", "error"}]}``.
  1060	
  1061	        Invariants whose SQL starts with ``SELECT`` are evaluated directly and
  1062	        the first column of the first row is treated as truthy/falsy. Other SQL
  1063	        is treated as an expression: aggregate expressions are evaluated once
  1064	        for the table while non-aggregate expressions must be true for every
  1065	        row.
  1066	        """
  1067	        failures = []
  1068	        invariants = self.list_import_invariants(table)
  1069	        table_exists = self.table(table).exists()
  1070	        count = self.table(table).count if table_exists else 0
  1071	        for invariant in invariants:
  1072	            expression = invariant["expression"]
  1073	            ok = True
  1074	            try:
  1075	                if expression.lstrip().upper().startswith("SELECT"):
  1076	                    rows = self.execute(expression).fetchall()
  1077	                    ok = bool(rows and rows[0][0])
  1078	                else:
  1079	                    rows = self.execute(
  1080	                        "SELECT ({}) AS _import_invariant FROM {}".format(
  1081	                            expression, quote_identifier(table)
  1082	                        )
  1083	                    ).fetchall()
  1084	                    if count == 0:
  1085	                        # Non-aggregate expressions are vacuously true for an
  1086	                        # empty table; aggregate expressions return one row.
  1087	                        ok = bool(rows and rows[0][0]) if rows else True
  1088	                    elif len(rows) == 1:
  1089	                        ok = bool(rows[0][0])
  1090	                    else:
  1091	                        ok = all(bool(row[0]) for row in rows)
  1092	            except Exception as e:
  1093	                failures.append(
  1094	                    {
  1095	                        "id": invariant["id"],
  1096	                        "expression": expression,
  1097	                        "error": str(e),
  1098	                    }
  1099	                )
  1100	                continue
  1101	            if not ok:
  1102	                failures.append(
  1103	                    {
  1104	                        "id": invariant["id"],
  1105	                        "expression": expression,
  1106	                        "error": "expression evaluated to false",
  1107	                    }
  1108	                )
  1109	        return {"valid": not failures, "failures": failures}
  1110	
  1111	    def _format_import_error_report(
  1112	        self,
  1113	        table: Optional[str],
  1114	        error: Optional[Exception],
  1115	        failures: List[Dict[str, Any]],
  1116	    ) -> str:
  1117	        bits = []
  1118	        if error is not None:
  1119	            bits.append("Import error: {}".format(error))
  1120	        if failures:
  1121	            bits.append("Import invariant validation failed for table '{}':".format(table))
  1122	            for failure in failures:
  1123	                bits.append(
  1124	                    "  {} {}: {}".format(
  1125	                        failure["id"],
  1126	                        failure["expression"],
  1127	                        failure.get("error") or "false",
  1128	                    )
  1129	                )
  1130	        return "\n".join(bits)
  1131	
  1132	    def safe_import(
  1133	        self,
  1134	        table: Optional[str],
  1135	        operation: Callable[[], None],
  1136	        strict: bool = False,
  1137	    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
  1138	        """
  1139	        Run ``operation`` inside a rollback checkpoint. On success the
  1140	        checkpoint is committed and ``{"success": True}`` is returned. On
  1141	        failure the database is rolled back to its pre-operation state.
  1142	
  1143	        If ``strict`` is ``True`` a failure raises an exception instead of
  1144	        returning a result dictionary. Invariant failures raise
  1145	        :class:`ImportInvariantError`.
  1146	        """
  1147	        return self._safe_import_operation(table, operation, strict=strict)

STEP 100 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && head -40 docs/changelog.rst
OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
.. _changelog:

===========
 Changelog
===========

.. _v3_39:

3.39 (2025-11-24)
-----------------

- Fixed a bug with ``sqlite-utils install`` when the tool had been installed using ``uv``. (:issue:`687`)
- The ```--functions``` argument now optionally accepts a path to a Python file as an alternative to a string full of code, and can be specified multiple times - see :ref:`cli_query_functions`.  (:issue:`659`)
- ``sqlite-utils`` now requires on Python 3.10 or higher.

.. _v4_0a1:

4.0a1 (2025-11-23)
------------------

- **Breaking change**: The ``db.table(table_name)`` method now only works with tables. To access a SQL view use ``db.view(view_name)`` instead. (:issue:`657`)
- The ``table.insert_all()`` and ``table.upsert_all()`` methods can now accept an iterator of lists or tuples as an alternative to dictionaries. The first item should be a list/tuple of column names. See :ref:`python_api_insert_lists` for details. (:issue:`672`)
- **Breaking change**: The default floating point column type has been changed from ``FLOAT`` to ``REAL``, which is the correct SQLite type for floating point values. This affects auto-detected columns when inserting data. (:issue:`645`)
- Now uses ``pyproject.toml`` in place of ``setup.py`` for packaging. (:issue:`675`)
- Tables in the Python API now do a much better job of remembering the primary key and other schema details from when they were first created. (:issue:`655`)
- **Breaking change**: The ``table.convert()`` and ``sqlite-utils convert`` mechanisms no longer skip values that evaluate to ``False``. Previously the ``--skip-false`` option was needed, this has been removed. (:issue:`542`)
- **Breaking change**: Tables created by this library now wrap table and column names in ``"double-quotes"`` in the schema. Previously they would use ``[square-braces]``. (:issue:`677`)
- The ``--functions`` CLI argument now accepts a path to a Python file in addition to accepting a string full of Python code. It can also now be specified multiple times. (:issue:`659`)
- **Breaking change:** Type detection is now the default behavior for the ``insert`` and ``upsert`` CLI commands when importing CSV or TSV data. Previously all columns were treated as ``TEXT`` unless the ``--detect-types`` flag was passed. Use the new ``--no-detect-types`` flag to restore the old behavior. The ``SQLITE_UTILS_DETECT_TYPES`` environment variable has been removed. (:issue:`679`)

.. _v4_0a0:

4.0a0 (2025-05-08)
------------------

- Upsert operations now use SQLite's ``INSERT ... ON CONFLICT SET`` syntax on all SQLite versions later than 3.23.1. This is a very slight breaking change for apps that depend on the previous ``INSERT OR IGNORE`` followed by ``UPDATE`` behavior. (:issue:`652`)
- Python library users can opt-in to the previous implementation by passing ``use_old_upsert=True`` to the ``Database()`` constructor, see :ref:`python_api_old_upsert`.
- Dropped support for Python 3.8, added support for Python 3.13. (:issue:`646`)
- ``sqlite-utils tui`` is now provided by the `sqlite-utils-tui <https://github.com/simonw/sqlite-utils-tui>`__ plugin. (:issue:`648`)
- Test suite now also runs against SQLite 3.23.1, the last version (from 2018-04-10) before the new ``INSERT ... ON CONFLICT SET`` syntax was added. (:issue:`654`)

END STEP

block = '''.. _v_unreleased:\n\nUnreleased\n----------\n\n- New safe import mode protects bulk imports against partial failure. Use ``--safe-mode`` with the ``insert``, ``upsert`` and ``bulk`` commands, or the Python API methods ``safe_bulk_insert()``, ``safe_bulk_upsert()``, ``import_csv(..., safe_mode=True)`` and ``import_json(..., safe_mode=True)``. Imports are wrapped in rollback checkpoints, persistent import invariants are validated after the writes, and the database is rolled back to its exact pre-operation state (including schema changes) if anything fails.\n- New Python API for managing rollback checkpoints and import invariants: ``enable_safe_import()``, ``disable_safe_import()``, ``create_import_checkpoint()``, ``commit_checkpoint()``, ``rollback_to_checkpoint()``, ``cleanup_checkpoint()``, ``add_import_invariant()``, ``remove_import_invariant()``, ``list_import_invariants()`` and ``validate_import_invariants()``.\n- New CLI commands: ``enable-safe-import``, ``disable-safe-import``, ``add-import-invariant``, ``remove-import-invariant``, ``list-import-invariants`` and ``validate-import-invariants``.\n\n'''
assert anchor in src

path = 'sqlite_utils/db.py'
src = open(path).read()

# Add ordered list in __init__
old = "        self._active_checkpoints = set()\n"
new = "        self._active_checkpoints = set()\n        self._active_checkpoint_order: List[str] = []\n"
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# _create_checkpoint: track order
old = """        self._active_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
        return checkpoint_id
"""
new = """        self._active_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
        self._active_checkpoint_order.append(checkpoint_id)
        return checkpoint_id
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# helper to remove tracking
old = """    def _require_active_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
"""
new = """    def _forget_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoint_order:
            self._active_checkpoint_order.remove(checkpoint_id)

    def _require_active_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# commit_checkpoint: use _forget
old = """        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
"""
new = """        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self._forget_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# rollback_to_checkpoint: invalidate inner checkpoints
old = """        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        quoted = quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
        self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
"""
new = """        self._require_active_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        quoted = quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quoted))
        # Any checkpoints created after this one were destroyed by the rollback
        try:
            index = self._active_checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
            for inner_id in self._active_checkpoint_order[index + 1:]:
                self._active_checkpoints.discard(inner_id)
            self._active_checkpoint_order = self._active_checkpoint_order[: index + 1]
        except ValueError:
            pass
        self._forget_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
        self._finalized_checkpoints.add(checkpoint_id)
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# cleanup_checkpoint: use _forget
old = """            self._active_checkpoints.discard(checkpoint_id)
